 349:     def free_run(self, seconds):
 350:         mark = len(self.buf)
 351:         b, n = b"continue\n", 0
 352:         try:
 353:             while n < len(b):
 354:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
 355:         except OSError:
 356:             self.dead = True
 357:             return False, 0.0
 358:         t0 = time.time()
 359:         while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
 360:             if not self.rd(0.25):

 362:         try:
 363:             os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
 364:         except OSError:
 365:             self.dead = True
 366:             return False, time.time() - t0

 371:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)

 374:             ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
 375:             t1 = time.time()

 378:     def read_words(self, addr, n):
 379:         mark = len(self.buf)
 380:         if not self.send("dump 0x%08x 0x%08x" % (addr, addr + 4 * n)):
 381:             return None
 382:         out = {}
 383:         for line in self.buf[mark:].splitlines():
 384:             m = re.match(r"\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})\s+((?:[0-9a-f]{8}\s+){1,4})", line)
 385:             if not m:
 386:                 continue
 387:             base = int(m.group(1), 16)
 388:             for i, w in enumerate(m.group(2).split()):
 389:                 out[base + 4 * i] = struct.unpack("<I", struct.pack(">I", int(w, 16)))[0]
 390:         vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
 391:         return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals

 393:     def regs(self, which=(3, 5, 6, 7)):
 394:         mark = len(self.buf)
 395:         if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
 396:             return {}
 397:         seg, got = self.buf[mark:], {}
 398:         for k in which:
 399:             m = re.search(r"\br%d\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % k, seg)
 400:             if m:
 401:                 got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
 402:         return got

 404:     def close(self, patience=5.0):
 405:         try:
 406:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
 407:         except OSError:
 408:             pass
 409:         # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
 410:         deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
 411:         while True:
 412:             left = deadline - time.monotonic()
 413:             if left <= 0 or not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
 414:                 break
 415:         try:
 416:             os.close(self.fd)
 417:         except OSError:
 418:             pass
 419:         try:
 420:             _, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
 421:         except OSError:
 422:             st = None
 423:         code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
 424:         sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
 425:         return dict(exit=code, signal=sig)

 428: def plant(s, prog):
 429:     for off, v in CONSTS.items():
 430:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, v))
 431:     for off in OUTS:
 432:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, POISON))
 433:     for off, word, _t, _c in prog:
 434:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + off, word))
 435:     s.send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)

 438: def spell_ok(expected, got):
 439:     """The emulator's own spelling differs cosmetically from the generator's:
 440:     it prints `ldr r5,[r4]` for a zero offset and `#0` rather than `#0x0`.  Those
 441:     are FORMAT differences, not encoding differences -- the WORD is compared
 442:     separately and exactly.  Normalise both sides so the check keeps its teeth
 443:     (a wrong register or a wrong offset still fails) without red rows for
 444:     punctuation."""
 445:     def norm(t):
 446:         t = re.sub(r"\s+", "", t.lower())
 447:         t = re.sub(r",#0\]", "]", t)                      # [r4,#0]  -> [r4]
 448:         t = re.sub(r"#0x([0-9a-f]+)",
 449:                    lambda m: "#" + str(int(m.group(1), 16)), t)   # #0x100 -> #256
 450:         return t
 451:     return norm(expected) == norm(got)

 453: 
 454: def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
 455:     planted = {CODE + off: (w, t) for off, w, t, _ in prog}
 456:     bad, covered, ex = [], set(), []
 457:     for _ in range(n):
 458:         mark = len(s.buf)
 459:         if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
 460:             bad.append("host died / no prompt at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
 461:             break
 462:         m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", s.buf[mark:])
 463:         if not m:
 464:             bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
 465:             break
 466:         addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
 467:         got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
 468:         ex.append((addr, word, got))
 469:         if addr not in planted:
 470:             bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, never planted" % addr)
 471:             continue
 472:         pw, pt = planted[addr]
 473:         covered.add(addr)
 474:         if word != pw:
 475:             bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
 476:         if pt is not None and not spell_ok(pt, got):
 477:             bad.append("0x%08x %08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
 478:     return bad, covered, ex

 481: def run_arm(label, mmu_on=False, disable=False, accrue=None, max_drain_s=30.0):
 482:     accrue = ACCRUE_S if accrue is None else accrue
 483:     prog = build(mmu_on=mmu_on, disable_during_accrual=disable)
 484:     s = Session()
 485:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
 486:         return dict(label=label, status="NO-PROMPT", **s.close())
 487:     plant(s, prog)
 488:     bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
 489:     #  ACCRUAL.  The SIGALRM timer is stopped at the debugger prompt
 490:     #  (debugger.c:800) and restarted on `continue` (:840), so only free-run
 491:     #  seconds accrue -- which is what makes rate*T predictable at all.
 492:     ok, dt = s.free_run(accrue)
 493:     accrued = dt
 494:     mid = s.read_words(DATA + O_DRAIN, 5) if ok and not s.dead else None
 495:     #  PHASE CHANGE: the harness, not a guest clock, decides when accrual ends.
 496:     if ok and not s.dead:
 497:         s.send("pc=0x%08x" % DRAIN_ENTRY)
 498:     #  DRAIN, in slices, until PHASE says done.
 499:     t0, phase, out = time.time(), None, None
 500:     while ok and not s.dead and time.time() - t0 < max_drain_s:
 501:         ok, _ = s.free_run(0.5)
 502:         if not ok or s.dead:
 503:             break
 504:         out = s.read_words(DATA + O_DRAIN, 5)
 505:         if out is None:
 506:             break
 507:         phase = out[4]
 508:         if phase in (3, 4):
 509:             break
 510:     r = s.regs()
 511:     status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else ("SURVIVED" if ok else "INTERRUPT-LOST")
 512:     end = s.close()
 513:     return dict(label=label, status=status, regs=r, badis=bad, executed=ex,
 514:                 covered=cov, want={CODE + o for o, *_ in prog},
 515:                 accrued=accrued, mid=mid, out=out, phase=phase,
 516:                 drain_s=time.time() - t0, **end)
 517: 
 518: 
 519: #  Arm E walks the drain body in SEGMENTS, each entered by setting pc directly.
 520: #
 521: #  WHY SEGMENTS AND NOT ONE WALK, stated because the first draft did the obvious
 522: #  thing and got 7 offsets of silent non-coverage: under `step 1` the emulated CPU
 523: #  advances ONE cycle per command, and DEVICE_TICK is driven by the dyntrans cycle
 524: #  counter at one call per 1 << DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT == 16384 cycles.  So a
 525: #  single-stepped SPIN loop can never see a delivered tick inside a few dozen steps
 526: #  and the TICKSEEN arm is unreachable by stepping, however large the backlog is.
 527: #
 528: #  This check is about DECODE, not about control flow: it asks whether the emulator
 529: #  reads back each planted word as the instruction the assembler above meant.  That
 530: #  question is answered wherever the word executes, so entering a segment at its head
 531: #  is legitimate here -- and it is NOT a substitute for the behavioural evidence,
 532: #  which is separate: reaching phase 3 with a non-zero drained count is only possible
 533: #  if 0xac..0xc4 and 0xc8..0xd4 executed for real, in order, during arms D and X.
 534: E_SEGMENTS = ((0x70, 12),        # re-enable NOPs, freeze, PHASE=1, DLOOP, SPIN head
 535:               (0xa0, 2),         # subs fp / bne SPIN
 536:               (0xa8, 1),         # b DONE -- the budget-expiry arm
 537:               (0xac, 7),         # TICKSEEN .. b DLOOP
 538:               (0xc8, 5),         # DONE .. HALT
 539:               (0xdc, 2),         # OVER
 540:               (0xe4, 1))         # b HALT
 541: 
 542: 
 543: def run_drainenc():
 544:     """Arm E: every word of the drain body stepped as it EXECUTES."""
 545:     prog = build()
 546:     s = Session()
 547:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
 548:         return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", badis=[], covered=set(), nsteps=0,
 549:                     executed=[], **s.close())
 550:     plant(s, prog)
 551:     bad, cov, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
 552:     for head, n in E_SEGMENTS:
 553:         if s.dead:
 554:             break
 555:         s.send("pc=0x%08x" % (CODE + head))
 556:         b2, c2, e2 = step_and_check(s, prog, n)
 557:         bad += b2
 558:         cov |= c2
 559:         ex += e2
 560:     status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else "SURVIVED"
 561:     end = s.close()
 562:     return dict(status=status, badis=bad, covered=cov, nsteps=len(ex), executed=ex, **end)
 563: 
 564: 
 565: def run_kill():
 566:     prog = build(kill=True)
 567:     s = Session()
 568:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
 569:         return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", saw=False, **s.close())
 570:     plant(s, prog)
 571:     step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N + 3)
 572:     died = s.dead
 573:     if not died:
 574:         s.send("reg", timeout=10)
 575:         died = s.dead
 576:     end = s.close()
 577:     saw = "ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf
 578:     return dict(status="HOST-DIED" if died else "SURVIVED", saw=saw, **end)

 701:     #  *** A POISONED DRAINED-COUNT IS NOT A HUGE BACKLOG, IT IS A DEAD CLOCK, and

 706:     #  one this whole round is about, so conflating them would have shipped a wrong
 707:     #  record about the exact symptom under test.  Both still FAIL; they just have to
 708:     #  fail by their own names.  A live fix can never land here: the reset leaves the
 709:     #  counter at 1, so at least one tick is always owed.  ***
 710:     def backlog(A, ran):
 711:         if not ran:
 712:             return None, False
 713:         v = A["out"][0]
 714:         return (0, True) if v == POISON else (v, False)

 225:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
 226:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
 227:         if self.fd not in r:
 228:             return True
 229:         try:
 230:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
 231:         except OSError:
 232:             self.dead = True
 233:             return False
 234:         if not d:
 235:             self.dead = True
 236:             return False
 237:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
 238:         return True
 239: 
 240:     #  #467: an INACTIVITY deadline, not a total-time one.  This used to read
 241:     #  `t = time.time()` once and loop `while time.time() - t < timeout`, so a boot
 242:     #  that normally takes 40 s and took 90 s on a LOADED HOST failed -- while bytes
 243:     #  were arriving the whole time.  Slow was indistinguishable from stuck, and the
 244:     #  battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
 245:     #
 246:     #  `rd()` cannot report progress: it returns True both when data arrives and when
 247:     #  its select() times out quietly.  So progress is `len(self.buf)` changing.
 248:     #
 249:     #  STRICTLY MORE PERMISSIVE for a run that produces output, IDENTICAL for one that
 250:     #  does not -- it cannot turn a passing run into a failing one.  `timeout` now
 251:     #  means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth failing on.
 252:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
 253:         t = time.time()
 254:         seen = len(self.buf)
 255:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
 256:             if not self.rd():
 257:                 return False
 258:             if len(self.buf) != seen:
 259:                 seen = len(self.buf)
 260:                 t = time.time()
 261:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
 262:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
 263:                 continue
 264:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
 265:                 return True
 266:         return False
 267: 
 268:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
 269:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
 270:         _mark = len(self.buf)
 271:         n = 0
 272:         try:
 273:             while n < len(b):
 274:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
 275:         except OSError:
 276:             self.dead = True
 277:             return False
 278:         return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
 279: 
 280:     def free_run(self, seconds):
 281:         """`continue`, drain for `seconds`, then SIGINT back to the prompt.
 282: 
 283:         Returns (ok, measured_free_run_seconds).  The pty is DRAINED throughout: a full
 284:         pty buffer blocks the emulator, which would look like a device that stopped
 285:         ticking -- the exact conclusion this probe is trying to reach, so it must not be
 286:         manufacturable by the harness."""
 287:         mark = len(self.buf)
 288:         b, n = b"continue\n", 0
 289:         try:
 290:             while n < len(b):
 291:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
 292:         except OSError:
 293:             self.dead = True
 294:             return False, 0.0
 295:         t0 = time.time()
 296:         while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
 297:             if not self.rd(0.25):
 298:                 return False, time.time() - t0
 299:         try:
 300:             os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
 301:         except OSError:
 302:             self.dead = True
 303:             return False, time.time() - t0
 304:         ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
 305:         t1 = time.time()
 306:         if not ok and not self.dead:
 307:             try:
 308:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
 309:             except OSError:
 310:                 pass
 311:             ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
 312:             t1 = time.time()
 313:         return ok, t1 - t0
 314: 
 315:     def read_words(self, addr, n):
 316:         """Read n consecutive words out of RAM.  `dump` prints the raw little-endian
 317:         bytes, so a guest word 0x11223344 comes back as `44332211` and MUST be swapped."""
 318:         mark = len(self.buf)
 319:         if not self.send("dump 0x%08x 0x%08x" % (addr, addr + 4 * n)):
 320:             return None
 321:         out = {}
 322:         for line in self.buf[mark:].splitlines():
 323:             m = re.match(r"\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})\s+((?:[0-9a-f]{8}\s+){1,4})", line)
 324:             if not m:
 325:                 continue
 326:             base = int(m.group(1), 16)
 327:             for i, w in enumerate(m.group(2).split()):
 328:                 raw = int(w, 16)
 329:                 out[base + 4 * i] = struct.unpack("<I", struct.pack(">I", raw))[0]
 330:         vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
 331:         return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals
 332: 
 333:     def regs(self, which=(0, 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9)):
 334:         mark = len(self.buf)
 335:         if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
 336:             return {}
 337:         seg = self.buf[mark:]
 338:         names = {10: "sl", 11: "fp", 12: "ip", 13: "sp", 14: "lr"}
 339:         got = {}
 340:         for k in which:
 341:             nm = names.get(k, "r%d" % k)
 342:             m = re.search(r"\b%s\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % nm, seg)
 343:             if m:
 344:                 got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
 345:         return got
 346: 
 347:     def close(self, patience=5.0):
 348:         try:
 349:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
 350:         except OSError:
 351:             pass
 352:         # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
 353:         deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
 354:         while True:
 355:             left = deadline - time.monotonic()
 356:             if left <= 0 or not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
 357:                 break
 358:         try:
 359:             os.close(self.fd)
 360:         except OSError:
 361:             pass
 362:         try:
 363:             _, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
 364:         except OSError:
 365:             st = None
 366:         code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
 367:         sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
 368:         return dict(exit=code, signal=sig)

 370: 
 371: def plant(s, prog):
 372:     s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (LIVE, 0x11223344))
 373:     s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (SRC, 0x12345678))
 374:     for a in (CLOB, ITER, LASTV, LASTID):
 375:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (a, POISON))
 376:     for off, word, _t, _c in prog:
 377:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + off, word))
 378:     s.send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)
 379: 
 380: 
 381: def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
 382:     """`step 1` n times; check every EXECUTED word against what was planted at that
 383:     address AND its disassembly against the emulator's own spelling.  Returns
 384:     (mismatches, covered_addresses, executed)."""
 385:     planted = {CODE + off: (word, text) for off, word, text, _ in prog}
 386:     bad, covered, executed = [], set(), []
 387:     for _ in range(n):
 388:         mark = len(s.buf)
 389:         if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
 390:             bad.append("host died or no prompt during step %d" % (len(executed) + 1))
 391:             break
 392:         seg = s.buf[mark:]
 393:         m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", seg)
 394:         if not m:
 395:             bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(executed) + 1))
 396:             break
 397:         addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
 398:         got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
 399:         executed.append((addr, word, got))
 400:         if addr not in planted:
 401:             bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, which was never planted" % addr)
 402:             continue
 403:         pw, pt = planted[addr]
 404:         covered.add(addr)
 405:         if word != pw:
 406:             bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
 407:         if pt is not None and got != pt:
 408:             bad.append("0x%08x 0x%08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
 409:     return bad, covered, executed
 410: 
 411: 
 412: def run_witness(head, sentinel, slices, slice_s, label):
 413:     prog = head + prologue(sentinel[0], sentinel[1]) + LOOP + [LANDING]
 414:     s = Session()
 415:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
 416:         return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", label=label, samples=[], regs={}, badis=[],
 417:                     catchup=0, **s.close())
 418:     plant(s, prog)
 419:     bad, _, _ = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N)
 420:     r = s.regs()
 421:     samples, t_cum, lost = [], 0.0, False
 422:     for _ in range(slices):
 423:         ok, dt = s.free_run(slice_s)
 424:         t_cum += dt
 425:         if not ok or s.dead:
 426:             lost = True
 427:             break
 428:         w = s.read_words(CLOB, 4)
 429:         if w is None:
 430:             lost = True
 431:             break
 432:         samples.append(dict(t=t_cum, clob=w[0], iters=w[1], lastv=w[2], lastid=w[3]))
 433:     if s.dead:
 434:         status = "HOST-DIED"
 435:     elif lost:
 436:         status = "INTERRUPT-LOST"
 437:     else:
 438:         status = "SURVIVED"
 439:     catchup = s.buf.count(TIMERMSG)
 440:     end = s.close()
 441:     return dict(status=status, label=label, regs=r, badis=bad,
 442:                 samples=samples, catchup=catchup, **end)
 443: 
 444: 
 445: def run_kill():
 446:     prog = MMU_OFF + prologue(*SENT_A) + [KILL, (0x4c, NOP, NOPTXT, "")]
 447:     s = Session()
 448:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
 449:         return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", saw_fatal=False, badis=[], nsteps=0, **s.close())
 450:     plant(s, prog)
 451:     bad, _, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N + 1)
 452:     died = s.dead
 453:     if not died:
 454:         #  The exit may land between the write and our read; one more poke settles it.
 455:         s.send("reg", timeout=10)
 456:         died = s.dead
 457:     end = s.close()
 458:     seen = "ENABLE CLEAR" in s.buf
 459:     return dict(status="HOST-DIED" if died else "SURVIVED", saw_fatal=seen,
 460:                 badis=bad, nsteps=len(ex), **end)
 461: 
 462: 
 463: def run_encodings():
 464:     """Arm E: sentinel 0 makes the poll compare EQUAL, so `step 1` walks BOTH loop
 465:     bodies and every planted word is checked as it EXECUTES."""
 466:     prog = MMU_OFF + prologue(*SENT_E) + LOOP + [LANDING]
 467:     s = Session()
 468:     if not s.wait(timeout=120):
 469:         return dict(status="NO-PROMPT", regs={}, badis=[], covered=set(), want=set(),
 470:                     nsteps=0, executed=[], **s.close())
 471:     plant(s, prog)
 472:     bad, covered, ex = step_and_check(s, prog, PROLOGUE_N + 2 * len(LOOP))
 473:     r = s.regs()
 474:     want = {CODE + off for off, *_ in prog if off != LANDING[0]}
 475:     status = "HOST-DIED" if s.dead else "SURVIVED"
 476:     end = s.close()
 477:     return dict(status=status, regs=r, badis=bad, covered=covered, want=want,
 478:                 nsteps=len(ex), executed=ex, **end)

 481: def main():
 482:     t_probe = time.time()
 483:     print("=" * 78)
 484:     print("fbpending witness -- %s" % BIN)

 487:     print("  arm A: %d slices x %.1f s = %.0f s of FREE-RUNNING guest execution"

 554:     last_frozen_t = frozen[-1][2] if frozen else None
 555:     #  Ticks are linear in free-run time before the wrap, so the plateau height divided

 564:     ok = True

 566:     def row(tag, good, detail):

  94: int arm_cpu_new(struct cpu *cpu, struct memory *mem,
  95: 	struct machine *machine, int cpu_id, char *cpu_type_name)
  96: {
  97: 	int i, found;
  98: 	struct arm_cpu_type_def cpu_type_defs[] = ARM_CPU_TYPE_DEFS;
  99: 
 100: 	/*  Scan the list for this cpu type:  */
 101: 	i = 0; found = -1;
 102: 	while (i >= 0 && cpu_type_defs[i].name != NULL) {
 103: 		if (strcasecmp(cpu_type_defs[i].name, cpu_type_name) == 0) {
 104: 			found = i;
 105: 			break;
 106: 		}
 107: 		i++;
 108: 	}
 109: 	if (found == -1)
 110: 		return 0;
 111: 
 112: 	cpu->run_instr = arm_run_instr;
 113: 	cpu->memory_rw = arm_memory_rw;

 119: 
 120: 	cpu->cd.arm.cpu_type = cpu_type_defs[found];
 121: 	cpu->name            = strdup(cpu->cd.arm.cpu_type.name);
 122: 	cpu->is_32bit        = 1;
 123: 	cpu->byte_order      = EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
 124:

 134: 		cpu->cd.arm.control |= ARM_CONTROL_S;
 135: 	} else {
 136: 		cpu->cd.arm.cpsr |= ARM_MODE_SVC32;
 137: 		cpu->cd.arm.control |= ARM_CONTROL_R;
 138: 	}
 139: 
 140: 	/*  #291: the cache-size fields are 3 bits wide and encode the size as
 141: 	    log2(bytes) - 9, but 32 of the entries in arm_cpu_types.h leave
 142: 	    dcache_shift at 0 (ARM1136JSR1, which the rpi machine selects, is
 143: 	    one). Those computed 0 - 9 = -9 and shifted it left, which is

 782: 			    cpu->cpu_id, (int) (i << 12),

 611:     ratio = (S[0]["iters"] / S[0]["clob"]) if (S and S[0]["clob"]) else 0
 612:     row("D1 the ticks come from the DEVICE, not an absent read",
 613:         bool(S) and S[0]["clob"] > 0 and ratio >= 100,
 614:         "iters/ticks in slice 1 = %.0f (an unmapped read gives ~1)" % ratio)
 615:     row("L1 the guest never stopped running",
 616:         bool(deltas) and all(d[4] > 0 for d in deltas),
 617:         "min d(iters) over %d slices = %s"
 421: 
 422: 	x = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;
 423: 	if (x < 8)
 424: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic1->irq_base + x;
 425: 	else
 426: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic2->irq_base + x - 8;
 427: 
 428: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 429: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 430: 
 431: 	return 1;
 432: }

 440: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_reset)
 441: {
 442: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
 443: 
 444: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 445: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 446: 		if (idata & 0x40) {
 447: 			debug("[ footbridge_reset: GP16: Halting. ]\n");
 448: 			/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) cpu->running=0 already halts cleanly;
 449: 			    the col-0 exit(1) was a debug hack that kills the host.  */
 450: 			cpu->running = 0;
 451: 		}
 452: 	}
 453: 
 454: 	return 1;
 455: }

 458: /*
 459:  *  The Footbridge PCI configuration space is implemented as a direct memory
 460:  *  space (i.e. not one port for addr and one port for data). This function
 461:  *  translates that into bus_pci calls.
 462:  */
 463: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_pci)
 464: {
 465: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
 466: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 467: 	int bus, dev, func, reg;
 468: 
 469: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 470: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN);
 471: 
 472: 	/*  Decompose the (direct) address into its components:  */
 473: 	bus_pci_decompose_1(relative_addr, &bus, &dev, &func, &reg);
 474: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pcibus, bus, dev, func, reg);
 475: 
 476: 	if (bus == 255) {
 477: 		fatal("[ footbridge DEBUG ERROR: bus 255 unlikely,"
 478: 		    " pc (might not be updated) = 0x%08x ]\n", (int)cpu->pc);
 479: 		/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) don't exit() the host on a guest PCI
 480: 		    access to the (unlikely) bus 255; treat it as handled.  */
 481: 		return 1;
 482: 	}
 483: 
 484: 	debug("[ footbridge pci: %s bus %i, device %i, function %i, register "
 485: 	    "%i ]\n", writeflag == MEM_READ? "read from" : "write to", bus,
 486: 	    dev, func, reg);
 487: 
 488: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pcibus, writeflag == MEM_READ?
 489: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
 490: 
 491: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 492: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN, odata);
 493: 
 494: 	return 1;
 495: }

 179: def br(cond, target, here):
 180:     off = ((target - here - 8) >> 2) & 0xFFFFFF
 181:     return (cond << 28) | 0x0A000000 | off, None

 183: 
 184: NOP = (0xE1A00000, "mov r0,r0")
 185: 
 186: 
 187: def build(mmu_on=False, disable_during_accrual=False, kill=False):
 188:     """Returns [(offset, word, expected_disassembly_or_None, comment)]."""
 189:     p = []
 190: 
 191:     def emit(off, wt, comment=""):
 192:         p.append((off, wt[0], wt[1], comment))
 193: 
 194:     if mmu_on:
 195:         for o in (0x00, 0x04, 0x08):
 196:             emit(o, NOP, "arm T: MMU LEFT ON -- the negative control")
 197:     else:
 198:         p.append((0x00, 0xEE110F10, "mrc 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "read CP15 c1"))
 199:         p.append((0x04, 0xE3C00001, "bic r0,r0,#1", "clear MMU enable"))
 200:         p.append((0x08, 0xEE010F10, "mcr 15,0,r0,cr1,cr0,0", "MMU OFF -> VA == PA"))
 201:     p.append((0x0c, 0xEE113F10, "mrc 15,0,r3,cr1,cr0,0", "C2: CP15 c1 read BACK"))
 202:     emit(0x10, mov("r0", FB), "footbridge base")
 203:     emit(0x14, mov("r4", DATA), "scratch RAM")
 204:     emit(0x18, ldr("r5", "r4", K_LIVE), "C1 liveness, expect 0x11223344")
 205:     emit(0x1c, ldr("r6", "r0", 0), "A1 VENDOR_ID, expect 0x1011")
 206:     emit(0x20, ldr("r2", "r4", K_SRC), "0x12345678 out of RAM")
 207:     emit(0x24, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "TIMER_x_LOAD <- 0x12345678")
 208:     emit(0x28, ldr("r7", "r0", T_LOAD), "A2 expect 0x00345678 (the handler's mask)")
 209:     emit(0x2c, ldr("r2", "r4", K_CON), "CTRL = TIMER_ENABLE|TIMER_FCLK_256")
 210:     emit(0x30, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL), "TIMER_1_CONTROL <- 0x88")
 211:     emit(0x34, ldr("r2", "r4", K_L), "load 256")
 212:     emit(0x38, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "TIMER_1_LOAD <- 256 => 762.94 Hz")
 213:     emit(0x3c, ldr("r8", "r4", K_SEN), "sentinel 0x00FFFFFF")
 214:     emit(0x40, ldr("ip", "r4", K_SPIN), "spin budget")
 215:     emit(0x44, mov("sl", 0), "drained count")
 216:     emit(0x48, mov("lr", 0), "accrual iterations")
 217:     emit(0x4c, mov("r1", 0), "")
 218:     emit(0x50, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 0")
 219:     if disable_during_accrual:
 220:         emit(0x54, ldr("r2", "r4", K_COFF), "arm X: CTRL = 0")
 221:         emit(0x58, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL),
 222:              "arm X: DISABLE.  Zeroes the counter ONCE and leaves the core timer running.")
 223:     else:
 224:         emit(0x54, NOP, "")
 225:         emit(0x58, NOP, "")
 226:     if kill:
 227:         p.append((0x5c, 0xE590718C, "ldr r7,[r0,#396]",
 228:                   "arm K: IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read -> fatal()+exit(1) (#539: was +0x188)"))
 229:         emit(0x60, NOP, "")
 230:         return p
 231:     #  accrual loop -- publishes liveness and a fresh VENDOR_ID every iteration
 232:     emit(0x5c, add1("lr"), "ACCRUE: iterations++")
 233:     emit(0x60, st("lr", "r4", O_ITER), "publish iterations")
 234:     emit(0x64, ldr("r1", "r0", 0), "fresh VENDOR_ID read, every iteration")
 235:     emit(0x68, st("r1", "r4", O_VEND), "publish it")
 236:     p.append((0x6c, br(0xE, CODE + 0x5c, CODE + 0x6c)[0], None, "b ACCRUE"))
 237:     #  ---- DRAIN entry.  The harness sets pc here after the accrual free-run. ----
 238:     if disable_during_accrual:
 239:         emit(0x70, ldr("r2", "r4", K_CON), "arm X: re-enable")
 240:         emit(0x74, st("r2", "r0", T_CTRL),
 241:              "arm X: CONTROL <- 0x88.  reload_timer_value() does NOT clear the counter.")
 242:     else:
 243:         emit(0x70, NOP, "")
 244:         emit(0x74, NOP, "")
 245:     emit(0x78, ldr("r2", "r4", K_FRZ), "0x00FFFFFF")
 246:     emit(0x7c, st("r2", "r0", T_LOAD), "FREEZE: LOAD <- 0x00FFFFFF => 0.0116 Hz")
 247:     emit(0x80, mov("r1", 1), "")
 248:     emit(0x84, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 1")
 249:     emit(0x88, ldr("sp", "r4", K_LIM), "drain limit")
 250:     emit(0x8c, st("r8", "r0", T_VALUE), "DLOOP: TIMER_1_VALUE <- sentinel")
 251:     emit(0x90, movr("fp", "ip"), "reset spin budget")
 252:     emit(0x94, ldr("r9", "r0", T_VALUE), "SPIN: read TIMER_1_VALUE")
 253:     emit(0x98, cmpr("r9", "r8"), "")
 254:     p.append((0x9c, br(0x1, CODE + 0xac, CODE + 0x9c)[0], None, "bne TICKSEEN"))
 255:     emit(0xa0, subs1("fp"), "")
 256:     p.append((0xa4, br(0x1, CODE + 0x94, CODE + 0xa4)[0], None, "bne SPIN"))
 257:     p.append((0xa8, br(0xE, CODE + 0xc8, CODE + 0xa8)[0], None,
 258:               "budget expired, sentinel intact -> backlog is 0"))
 259:     emit(0xac, st("r9", "r4", O_LASTV), "TICKSEEN: publish the value the tick wrote")
 260:     emit(0xb0, st("r1", "r0", T_CLEAR), "TIMER_1_CLEAR -- one decrement")
 261:     emit(0xb4, add1("sl"), "")
 262:     emit(0xb8, st("sl", "r4", O_DRAIN), "publish the drained count")
 263:     emit(0xbc, cmpr("sl", "sp"), "")
 264:     p.append((0xc0, br(0xA, CODE + 0xdc, CODE + 0xc0)[0], None, "bge OVER"))
 265:     p.append((0xc4, br(0xE, CODE + 0x8c, CODE + 0xc4)[0], None, "b DLOOP"))
 266:     emit(0xc8, mov("r1", 3), "DONE:")
 267:     emit(0xcc, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 3")
 268:     emit(0xd0, ldr("r1", "r0", 0), "final VENDOR_ID")
 269:     emit(0xd4, st("r1", "r4", O_VEND), "")
 270:     p.append((0xd8, br(0xE, CODE + 0xd8, CODE + 0xd8)[0], None, "HALT"))
 271:     emit(0xdc, mov("r1", 4), "OVER:")
 272:     emit(0xe0, st("r1", "r4", O_PHASE), "PHASE = 4 -- drain limit hit")
 273:     p.append((0xe4, br(0xE, CODE + 0xd8, CODE + 0xe4)[0], None, "b HALT"))
 274:     return p

  81: /*
  82:  *  memory_writemax64():
  83:  *
  84:  *  Write at most 64 bits of data to a buffer.  Length is given by
  85:  *  len, and the byte order by cpu->byte_order.
  86:  *
  87:  *  This function should not be called with cpu == NULL.
  88:  */
  89: void memory_writemax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len,
  90: 	uint64_t data)
  91: {
  92: 	int i, byte_order = cpu->byte_order;
  93: 
  94: 	if (len & MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN) {
  95: 		len &= ~MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
  96: 		byte_order = EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN;
  97: 	}
  98: 
  99: 	if (byte_order == EMUL_LITTLE_ENDIAN)
 100: 		for (i=0; i<len; i++) {
 101: 			buf[i] = data & 255;
 102: 			data >>= 8;
 103: 		}
 104: 	else
 105: 		for (i=0; i<len; i++) {
 106: 			buf[len - 1 - i] = data & 255;
 107: 			data >>= 8;
 108: 		}
 109: }

 112: /*
 113:  *  zeroed_alloc():
 114:  *
 115:  *  Allocates a block of memory using mmap(), and if that fails, try
 116:  *  malloc() + memset(). The returned memory block contains only zeroes.
 117:  */
 118: void *zeroed_alloc(size_t s)
 119: {
 120: 	void *p = mmap(NULL, s, PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE,
 121: 	    MAP_ANON | MAP_PRIVATE, -1, 0);
 122: 
 123: 	/*  #175: (Codex/Fable) mmap failure is MAP_FAILED, not NULL  */
 124: 	if (p == MAP_FAILED) {
 125: #if 1
 126: 		fprintf(stderr, "zeroed_alloc(): mmap() failed. This should"
 127: 		    " not usually happen. If you can reproduce this, then"
 128: 		    " please contact me with details about your run-time"
 129: 		    " environment.\n");
 130: 		exit(1);
 131: #else
 132: 		CHECK_ALLOCATION(p = malloc(s));
 133: 		memset(p, 0, s);
 134: #endif
 135: 	}
 136: 
 137: 	return p;
 138: }
 531:  *
 532:  *  Translate a physical address into a host address. The usual way to call
 533:  *  this function is to make sure that paddr is page aligned, which will result
 534:  *  in the host _page_ corresponding to that address.
 535:  *
 536:  *  Return value is a pointer to the address in the host, or NULL on failure.
 537:  *  On reads, a NULL return value should be interpreted as reading all zeroes.
 538:  */
 539: unsigned char *memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(struct memory *mem,
 540: 	uint64_t paddr, int writeflag)
 541: {
 542: 	void **table;
 543: 	int entry;
 544: 	const int mask = (1 << BITS_PER_PAGETABLE) - 1;
 545: 	const int shrcount = MAX_BITS - BITS_PER_PAGETABLE;
 546: 	unsigned char *hostptr;
 547: 
 548: 	table = (void **) mem->pagetable;
 549: 	entry = (paddr >> shrcount) & mask;
 550: 
 551: 	/*  printf("memory_paddr_to_hostaddr(): p=%16" PRIx64
 552: 	    " w=%i => entry=0x%x\n", (uint64_t) paddr, writeflag, entry);  */
 553: 
 554: 	if (table[entry] == NULL) {
 555: 		size_t alloclen;
 556: 
 557: 		/*
 558: 		 *  Special case:  reading from a nonexistant memblock
 559: 		 *  returns all zeroes, and doesn't allocate anything.
 560: 		 *  (If any intermediate pagetable is nonexistant, then
 561: 		 *  the same thing happens):
 562: 		 */
 563: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 564: 			return NULL;
 565: 
 566: 		/*  Allocate a memblock:  */
 567: 		alloclen = 1 << BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK;
 568: 
 569: 		/*  printf("  allocating for entry %i, len=%i\n",
 570: 		    entry, alloclen);  */
 571: 
 572: 		/*  Anonymous mmap() should return zero-filled memory,
 573: 		    try malloc + memset if mmap failed.  */
 574: 		table[entry] = (void *) mmap(NULL, alloclen,
 575: 		    PROT_READ | PROT_WRITE, MAP_ANON | MAP_PRIVATE, -1, 0);
 576: 		/*  #175: (Codex/Fable) mmap failure is MAP_FAILED, not NULL  */
 577: 		if (table[entry] == MAP_FAILED) {
 578: 			CHECK_ALLOCATION(table[entry] = malloc(alloclen));
 579: 			memset(table[entry], 0, alloclen);
 580: 		}
 581: 	}
 582: 
 583: 	hostptr = (unsigned char *) table[entry];
 584: 
 585: 	if (hostptr != NULL)
 586: 		hostptr += (paddr & ((1 << BITS_PER_MEMBLOCK) - 1));
 587: 
 588: 	return hostptr;
 589: }

MACHINE_SETUP(cats)
{
	struct ebsaboot ebsaboot;
	struct pci_data *pci_bus;
	char bs[300], tmpstr[400];
	int boot_id = machine->bootdev_id >= 0? machine->bootdev_id : 0;

	machine->machine_name = strdup("CATS evaluation board");

	if (machine->emulated_hz == 0)
		machine->emulated_hz = 50000000;

	if (machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 256)
		fprintf(stderr, "WARNING! Real CATS machines cannot"
		    " have more than 256 MB RAM. Continuing anyway.\n");

	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "footbridge irq=%s.cpu[%i].irq"
	    " addr=0x42000000", machine->path, machine->bootstrap_cpu);
	pci_bus = (struct pci_data *) device_add(machine, tmpstr);

	/*  DC21285_ROM_BASE (256 KB at 0x41000000)  */
	dev_ram_init(machine, 0x41000000, 256 * 1024, DEV_RAM_RAM, 0, NULL);

	/*  NetBSD, OpenBSD, and Linux (?) clean their caches here:  */
	dev_ram_init(machine, 0x50000000, 0x10000, DEV_RAM_RAM, 0, NULL);

	/*  Interrupt ack space?  */
	dev_ram_init(machine, 0x80000000, 0x1000, DEV_RAM_RAM, 0, NULL);

	/*  Linux uses 0xc0000000 as phys.:  */
	dev_ram_init(machine, 0xc0000000, 0x20000000, DEV_RAM_MIRROR, 0x0, NULL);

	/*  OpenBSD reboot needs 0xf??????? to be mapped to phys.:  */
	dev_ram_init(machine, 0xf0000000, 0x1000000, DEV_RAM_MIRROR, 0x0, NULL);

	bus_pci_add(machine, pci_bus, machine->memory, 0xc0, 8, 0, "s3_virge");

	if (!machine->prom_emulation)
		return;

	/*  See cyclone_boot.h for details.  */

	/*  DC21285_ROM_BASE "reboot" code:  (works with NetBSD)  */
	store_32bit_word(cpu, 0x41000008ULL, 0xef8c64ebUL);

	cpu->cd.arm.r[0] = /* machine->physical_ram_in_mb */
	    7 * 1048576 - 0x1000;

	memset(&ebsaboot, 0, sizeof(struct ebsaboot));
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_magic), BT_MAGIC_NUMBER_CATS);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_vargp), 0);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_pargp), 0);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_args), cpu->cd.arm.r[0]
	    + sizeof(struct ebsaboot));
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_l1), 7 * 1048576 - 32768);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_memstart), 0);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_memend),
	    machine->physical_ram_in_mb * 1048576);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_memavail), 7 * 1048576);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_fclk), 50 * 1000000);
	store_32bit_word_in_host(cpu, (unsigned char *)
	    &(ebsaboot.bt_pciclk), 66 * 1000000);
	/*  TODO: bt_vers  */
	/*  TODO: bt_features  */

	store_buf(cpu, cpu->cd.arm.r[0],
	    (char *)&ebsaboot, sizeof(struct ebsaboot));

	snprintf(bs, sizeof(bs), "(hd%i)%s root=/dev/wd%i%s%s",
	    boot_id, machine->boot_kernel_filename, boot_id,
	    (machine->boot_string_argument[0])? " " : "",
	    machine->boot_string_argument);

	store_string(cpu, cpu->cd.arm.r[0]+sizeof(struct ebsaboot), bs);

	arm_setup_initial_translation_table(cpu, 7 * 1048576 - 32768);
}


MACHINE_DEFAULT_CPU(cats)
{
	machine->cpu_name = strdup("SA110");
}


MACHINE_DEFAULT_RAM(cats)
{
	machine->physical_ram_in_mb = 64;
}


MACHINE_REGISTER(cats)
{
	MR_DEFAULT(cats, "CATS evaluation board", MACHINE_CATS);

	machine_entry_add_alias(me, "cats");

	me->set_default_ram = machine_default_ram_cats;
}

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Offline check: does `wait()` fail on SILENCE, or merely on ELAPSED TIME?

WHY THIS EXISTS.  Every pty probe in this tree waited on an ABSOLUTE deadline --
`t = time.time()` once, then `while time.time() - t < timeout`.  That budget is
spent by the CLOCK, not by the guest, so a boot that normally takes 40 s and takes
90 s on a loaded host FAILS -- while bytes were arriving steadily the whole time.
A run that is merely SLOW was indistinguishable from one that is STUCK, and the
battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.

#467 makes the deadline an INACTIVITY deadline: the clock resets whenever the
buffer grows.  `timeout` then means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth
failing on.

WHAT A PRINT CANNOT PROVE.  Reading the converted source tells you the assignment
is there; it does not tell you the LOOP honours it.  This file proves the
behaviour instead, against a stub in place of the emulator -- no rig, no build, no
wall-clock oracle of its own, so it is as load-insensitive as the property it
defends.

THE STRADDLE.  Two legs, and BOTH must hold or the conversion is worthless:

  SLOW-BUT-PROGRESSING   a stream that emits steadily and finishes well past the
                         deadline.  The OLD shape FAILS it -- that IS the defect
                         -- and the NEW shape must PASS.
  GENUINELY STUCK        a stream that emits nothing at all.  BOTH shapes must
                         FAIL.  This leg is the one that matters: a conversion
                         that made the timeout unreachable would sail through the
                         first leg and defend nothing.

The two legs are deliberately opposite in sign.  A change that passes only the
first has not fixed the timeout, it has REMOVED it.

NOT COVERED, and deliberately: `free_run(seconds)` is NOT converted anywhere.  It
is not a timeout -- it is an EXPOSURE WINDOW that runs the guest for a set period
and then SIGINTs.  Converting it would change the experiment rather than protect
it, and a naive sweep over `time.time()` sites would have eaten it.
"""
import io
import os
import re
import sys
import time

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))

#  The probes converted by #467.  A file listed here MUST carry the reset; a file
#  that loses it silently reverts to the absolute deadline, which is exactly the
#  regression this selftest exists to catch.
CONVERTED = ("fbpending_drain_probe.py", "fbpending_witness.py")

FAILS = 0


def check(label, got, want):
    global FAILS
    ok = (got == want)
    if not ok:
        FAILS += 1
    print("  %-46s %-10s %-10s %s" % (label, got, want, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))


class Stub(object):
    """Stands in for the pty side of a probe.  `chunks` is what arrives per tick;
    a None entry is a tick that produces nothing."""

    def __init__(self, chunks):
        self.buf = ""
        self.chunks = list(chunks)
        self.dead = False

    def rd(self, t=0.01):
        time.sleep(0.004)
        if self.chunks:
            c = self.chunks.pop(0)
            if c:
                self.buf += c
        return True


def wait_absolute(s, timeout):
    """The PRE-#467 shape, kept here as the negative control.  Do not 'fix' it."""
    t = time.time()
    while time.time() - t < timeout:
        if not s.rd():
            return False
        if s.buf.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
            return True
    return False


def wait_inactivity(s, timeout):
    """The #467 shape: the clock resets on progress, so `timeout` means SILENCE."""
    t = time.time()
    seen = len(s.buf)

**Q1 — premise audit**

- **(a) READ — yes.** [dev_footbridge.c, `IRQ_ENABLE_SET`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:578) preserves `odata = d->irq_enable`, removes the diagnostic and terminating call, and retains the common readback tail. No latch or replacement behavior was added. The supplied datasheet supports the comment: line 6826 identifies the read register as `IRQEnable`; lines 6913–6915 say “read-only” and “can only be changed by writing to the EnableSet and EnableClear registers”; line 6924 specifies “Reset value: 0.” Initialization also zeroes the field.

- **(b) READ — yes, 3/3 probes bind complaint text and exit 1.** The register header defines CLEAR at `0x18c`; the timer fold starts at `0x300`. The supplied instruction therefore reaches the CLEAR read branch unchanged. Sites requires `ENABLE CLEAR` in its captured diagnostics and exit 1. Drain and witness additionally require `HOST-DIED` and a surviving comparison arm. This establishes a temporary control against the current implementation; write-only hardware semantics do not justify terminating the host.

- **(c) READ + REASONED — teardown remains unbounded.** Both [drain `Session.close`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py:404) and [witness `Session.close`](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_witness.py:347) eventually call blocking `waitpid(pid, 0)`. A child that does not process `quit` and survives terminal closure—for example, with SIGHUP ignored or blocked—can hang the probe indefinitely. EOF also does not prove process termination. The five-second deadline bounds the drain loop only.
